# Malvi, Nimadi and Bagheli — overnight triage

**All 757 proposals approved and saved. Persian/Arabic loans are nested under their etymological entries; immediate transmission is unspecified.**

Deadline: September 11, 2026, 11:30 a.m. America/New_York (15:30 UTC). Proposals are numbered independently by survey. Each row groups only selected compatible responses; exact IDs, locality tags, source locators and assignment rows are in the corresponding JSON manifest.

Acceptance reconciled the frozen records against current IDs, meanings and overlay rows. All 3,777 links passed temporary graph validation. See [acceptance validation](acceptance-validation.json).

## Final research counts

| Survey | Proposals | Records / links | Held | Unexamined |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Malvi | 291 | 1043 / 1054 | 68 | 1017 |
| Nimadi | 230 | 1908 / 1932 | 50 | 868 |
| Bagheli | 236 | 780 / 791 | 46 | 795 |

# Malvi — batch 001

**20 proposals, approved and saved to the accepted overlay.**

## Straightforward

| # | Malvi | Proposed etymology | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **mātho, māthā, matha** ‘head’ | **masta**, [CDIAL 9926.1](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=571); ID `9926` | Pali mattha/matthaka and Prakrit mattha lead to Hindi māthā and explicitly Marwari mātho ‘head, forehead’. The dental aspirate and vowel length fit the first branch of masta/mastaka; no brain-related subsection is intended. |
| 3 | **bal, bāl, baḷ** ‘hair’ | **vā́la**, [CDIAL 11572](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=674); ID `11572` | CDIAL gives Hindi bāl, Gujarati vāḷ and Bhojpuri bār ‘hair’ beneath vāla. Initial v > b and the regional lateral variants have explicit comparanda. |
| 4 | **kān, kāṇ** ‘ear’ | **kárṇa**, [CDIAL 2830](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=144); ID `2830` | Prakrit kaṇṇa corresponds to Hindi, Old Marwari, Gujarati and Marathi kān ‘ear’ in CDIAL. The local dental/retroflex nasal and variable written vowel length fit this inherited family. |
| 5 | **dāt** ‘tooth’ | **dánta**, [CDIAL 6152](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=352); ID `6152` | CDIAL explicitly gives Hindi, Marwari, Gujarati and Marathi dā̃t from danta. Reduction of nt with vowel lengthening/nasalisation accounts for these forms; survey notation varies in whether nasalisation is written. |
| 6 | **jib** ‘tongue’ | **jihvā́**, [CDIAL 5228.1](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=288); ID `5228` | Prakrit jibbhā and Hindi jīb(h), Gujarati jībh and Marathi jībh are cited under jihvā. The reduced labial cluster fits the selected jib response. |
| 7 | **peṭ, peṭh, piṭ** ‘belly’ | **\*pēṭṭa**, [CDIAL 8376.1](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=475); ID `8376` | CDIAL’s first branch has Prakrit peṭṭa/piṭṭa and Hindi peṭ, Old Marwari peṭa, Gujarati peṭ; Maithili peṭ(h) also supplies an aspirated comparator. This is the e/i-vowel branch, separate from pōṭṭa. |
| 8 | **poṭ** ‘belly’ | **\*pōṭṭa**, [CDIAL 8376.3](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=475); ID `8376-3` | CDIAL’s third branch explicitly gives Prakrit poṭṭa/puṭṭa and Marathi poṭ ‘belly’. The o vowel motivates this specific subsection rather than the peṭṭa head. |
| 12 | **haḍḍā, haḍḍi, haḍ** ‘bone’ | **haḍḍa**, [CDIAL 13952](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=807); ID `13952` | Prakrit haḍḍa, Hindi haḍḍā/haḍḍī and hāṛ, and Gujarati/Marathi hāḍ give direct comparanda. Turner explicitly calls a connection to asthi very doubtful; the proposal stops at haḍḍa. |
| 14 | **gam, gām, gā̃v, gāv, gā̃** ‘village’ | **grā́ma**, [CDIAL 4368](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=235); ID `4368` | CDIAL lists Prakrit gāma, Gujarati gām, Marwari gā̃v and Hindi gā̃u beneath grāma. The nasal and v/u outcomes are directly represented, without assigning the separate dehāt or khelo responses. |
| 15 | **kuni, koni, kohni** ‘elbow’ | **kapʰōṇi**, [CDIAL 2757](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=138); ID `2757` | CDIAL gives Prakrit kuhaṇī, Hindi kohnī/kuhnī/kehunī, Gujarati kɔṇī and Nepali kuinu. These supply both the medial-h and contracted/metathesised comparanda for the local forms. |
| 16 | **ā̃kh, ānkh, ākh, āŋkh, aŋkh** ‘eye’ | **ákṣi**, [CDIAL 43](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=3); ID `43` | Prakrit akkhi/acchi corresponds to Hindi ā̃kh, Bhojpuri ākhⁱ and Old Marwari ākhi in CDIAL. Cluster reduction, vowel length and optional nasalisation fit the selected forms. |
| 17 | **nāk, nākh** ‘nose’ | **\*nakka**, [CDIAL 6909](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=397); ID `6909` | Prakrit ṇakka, Hindi/Gujarati/Marathi nāk and Kumaoni nākh are explicit comparanda under *nakka. Turner’s proposed deeper *nas-ka/*nast-ka origin is uncertain; the assignment stays at the attested comparative *nakka family. |
| 19 | **muh** ‘mouth’ | **múkʰa**, [CDIAL 10158](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=585); ID `10158` | Prakrit muha and Hindi muh/mũh, Bhojpuri mũh and Gujarati mõh appear under mukha ‘mouth, face’. The weakened kh and loss or retention of final h fit these selected mouth responses. |
| 20 | **hāth** ‘arm’ | **hásta**, [CDIAL 14024](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=811); ID `14024` | CDIAL explicitly gives Hindi/Marwari hāth ‘hand, arm, cubit’ and Marathi hāt. The arm/hand semantic range is primary-source evidence, and inherited st > tth > th/t explains the stem. |

## Qualified

| # | Malvi | Proposed etymology | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | **madho, madhā, mādā, mato, matā, mādo** ‘head’ | **masta**, [CDIAL 9926.1](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=571); ID `9926` | Compare the same survey’s māthā/mātho and CDIAL’s Marwari mātho, Gujarati māthũ. Voicing or loss of aspiration differentiates these local forms; the family is plausible, but these developments need locality-specific confirmation. |
| 9 | **uŋgəḷi, aŋgḷi, āŋgḷi, aŋgḷiya, aŋgaḷi, uŋgḷi, aŋgri, aŋgiḷi, aŋgəḷi, uŋgili, aŋgali** ‘finger’ | **aŋgúli**, [CDIAL 135.1](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=7); ID `135` | CDIAL lists Hindi uṅglī, Gujarati ā̃gḷī, Marathi ãgḷī and Old Hindi ā̃gurī under aṅguli/aṅguri. Syncope and l/ḷ/r variation support this branch; survey-specific extended endings are retained in the proposal. |
| 10 | **nakh, nukh** ‘fingernail’ | **\*nakkʰa**, [CDIAL 6914.2](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=397); ID `6914-2` | The retained kh is compared with CDIAL’s explicitly strengthened *nakkha branch (Prakrit ṇakkha, Bshk. nakh, Torwali nōkh), rather than the nah-/noh- reflexes of plain nakha. A learned or contact-supported retention cannot yet be excluded in these central varieties. |
| 11 | **caməḍo, caməḍi, camaḍa, camḍa, cāmaḍo, camḍi, caməda, cāmḍi, cāmaḍa** ‘skin’ | **\*carmaḍa-**, [CDIAL 4701, extension -ḍa-](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=255); ID `4701-2` | CDIAL gives Hindi camṛā, Gujarati cāmḍũ/cāmḍī and Marathi cāmḍẽ/cāmḍī as the historical -ḍ- extension of carman via camma. These survey forms fit that inherited extended family; the historical extension is supported, although local suffix productivity has not been established. Jambu has a dedicated historical extension node *carmaḍa- (4701-2), which is the selected parent. |
| 13 | **haḍəka, haḍikā, haḍiko, haḍəko, haḍəki, haḍiki, haḍikyo, haḍikyā, haḍikya, haḍika** ‘bone’ | **haḍḍa**, [CDIAL 13952](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=807); ID `13952` | The base agrees with haḍḍa; CDIAL’s addenda explicitly provide k-extended haṛkɔ, hāḍkī and Garhwali hāḍgu (< *hāḍku?). The survey k-forms fit this extended family, with the exact age/productivity of the suffix left open. |
| 18 | **hatheḷi, hateḷi, hatəli, hateli, hateri, hatheli, hatəḷi** ‘palm’ | **hastatala**, [CDIAL 14029](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=811); ID `14029` | CDIAL gives Pali hatthatala and Hindi/Punjabi hathelī from hastatala ‘palm’. The contracted local forms fit that lexicalised compound, but Turner marks Gujarati hathelī as a Hindi loan, so the immediate inheritance/contact route remains qualified. |

20 proposals; 79 affected records; 79 saved assignment rows: 79 `reflex`. Historical extensions and uncertain contact pathways are explained in the evidence.

Excluded responses remain held in the cumulative review and holds.json. All approved donor dependencies are resolved.

# Malvi — batch 002

**13 proposals, approved and saved to the accepted overlay.**

## Straightforward

| # | Malvi | Proposed etymology | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 21 | **ghar, ghare** ‘house’ | **gʰara**, [CDIAL 4428](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=239); ID `4428` | Pali/Prakrit *ghara* and Hindi, Marwari, Gujarati and Marathi *ghar* directly match the house stem. Turner calls the deeper derivation from *gṛha* phonetically difficult; the proposal therefore stops at *ghara*. |
| 22 | **chapara, chappar, capar** ‘roof’ | **cʰattvará**, [CDIAL 4976](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=272); ID `4976` | Compare Hindi *chappar/chaprā* ‘sloping thatch, hut’, Gujarati *chāprũ* ‘thatched roof’ and the article’s Punjabi *chappar*. The local vowels and optional written aspiration fit the same roof word under *chattvara*. |
| 23 | **kimāḍ, kamāḍ, kamaḍ, kimaḍə, kivaḍ, kũvāḍ** ‘door’ | **kavāṭa**, [CDIAL 2963](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=151); ID `2963` | CDIAL lists Hindi *kiwāṛ/kewāṛ*, Old Marwari *kibāṛi*, Gujarati *kamāṛ* and Marathi *kavāḍ*. These supply both the v/w and m variants and the retroflex stop/flap outcomes of *kavāṭa*. |
| 25 | **ukhaḷa, ukhaḷo, ukkḷa, okhəli, okhḷi, okhəḷi, okhaḷi, okəḷi, ukaḷi, ukhaḷi, ukhəḷ** ‘mortar’ | **\*udukkʰala**, [CDIAL 2360.4](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=111); ID `2360-4` | CDIAL’s fourth branch has Prakrit *ukkhala/okkhala*, Hindi *ūkhal/okhlī*, Gujarati/Marathi *ukhaḷ* and Bihari *okhar*. These match the contracted mortar forms, rather than the distinct uncontracted *ulūkhala* branches. |
| 26 | **musḷi, musaḷa, musəḷo, musaḷo, musaḷi, musaro, musli, musrā, musḷo, musḷa, musəḷa, musāḷ** ‘pestle’ | **músala**, [CDIAL 10223.1](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=589); ID `10223` | CDIAL’s s-branch gives Prakrit *musala*, Hindi *musal/mūslī*, Gujarati *musḷũ*, Marathi *musaḷ* and Bihari *mūsar*. The l/ḷ/r and contracted forms are directly comparable, with no inference to the separate retroflex-sibilant branch. |
| 27 | **hatoḍi, hathoḍo, hathāḍi, hatoḍā, həthoḍi, atoḍi, hātoḍa** ‘hammer’ | **\*hastakūṭa**, [CDIAL 14028](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=811); ID `14028` | CDIAL’s *hastakūṭa* article gives Hindi *hathauṛā/-ṛī*, Gujarati *hathɔṛɔ/athɔṛɔ* and Marathi *hatoḍā/-ḍī*. These directly support both the au/o contraction and the h-less Malvi variant; the forms continue the lexicalised compound. |
| 29 | **suy, sui** ‘needle’ | **sūcī́**, [CDIAL 13551.1](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=781); ID `13551` | Prakrit *sūī*, Hindi/Bihari *sūī*, Gujarati *soy* and Marathi *sūī* are explicitly in CDIAL’s first branch. The c-loss and resulting vowel sequence support these forms, separately from *suji* or nasalised c-retaining *sūñcī*. |
| 31 | **cānd, cand, cād** ‘moon’ | **candrá**, [CDIAL 4661.2](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=252); ID `4661` | CDIAL’s moon sense gives Prakrit *caṃda*, Hindi/Gujarati *cā̃d*, Hindi *candā* and Bhojpuri *cān*. The reduced cluster and nasal/stop variants support the selected forms; longer *candrama* responses are not included. |
| 32 | **tāro, tārā, tara** ‘star’ | **tāraká**, [CDIAL 5798.4](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=330); ID `5798-4` | CDIAL puts Hindi/Marathi *tārā* and Gujarati *tārɔ* specifically in its masculine *tāraka* branch, rather than feminine *tārā*. The selected western forms match that branch directly. |
| 33 | **pāni, pāṇi, paṇi** ‘water’ | **pānī́ya**, [CDIAL 8082](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=456); ID `8082` | Prakrit *pāṇia* and Hindi *pānī*, Marwari/Gujarati/Marathi *pāṇī* directly support the stem. The inherited vowel contraction and nasal variants match the selected forms. |

## Qualified

| # | Malvi | Proposed etymology | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 24 | **lakkaḍ, lakəḍa, lakədi, lakaḍa, lekaḍ, lakəḍi, lakaḍi, lākaḍā, lakiḍya, lakəda** ‘firewood’ | **\*lakkuṭa**, [CDIAL 10875.2](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=629); ID `10875-2` | The retained velar belongs to CDIAL’s second branch: Prakrit *lakkuḍa*, Hindi *lakṛī* ‘firewood’, Gujarati *lākṛũ* and Marathi *lākuḍ*. The plain *lakuṭa* branch instead has weakened *laur/lauṛ-*; regional contact remains possible, especially in the eastern forms. |
| 28 | **kurāḍi, karāḍi, kulaḍi, karaḍi, kuraḍi, kulhāḍi, karāḍo** ‘axe’ | **kuṭʰāra**, [CDIAL 3244](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=166); ID `3244` | CDIAL connects Prakrit *kuhāḍa/-ḍī*, Hindi *kulhāṛī*, Bihari *kulhārī* and Marathi *kurhāḍī* with *kuṭhāra*. The r/l variants and rearranged aspiration are supported; regional variants with lost aspiration remain within this qualified family. |
| 30 | **kapaḍo, kapaḍa, kapəḍā** ‘cloth’ | **karpaṭa**, [CDIAL 2871](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=146); ID `2871` | CDIAL has Prakrit *kappaḍa*, Marwari *kapṛo* and Marathi *kāpaḍ* beneath *karpaṭa*. The western forms fit this family; Turner flags borrowing for several neighbouring varieties, so immediate inheritance versus diffusion remains qualified. |

# Malvi — batch 003

## Straightforward

| 34 | **āg** ‘fire’ | **agní**, [CDIAL 55](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=3); ID `55` | CDIAL explicitly gives Prakrit *aggi*, Old Marwari/Gujarati *āgi*, Hindi *āg/āgī* and Bhojpuri *āgī*. Simplification of gn through gg, with lengthening or retained final i, supports this fire family. |
| 35 | **dhua, dhuā̃, dhũo, dũva** ‘smoke’ | **dʰūmá**, [CDIAL 6849.1](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=392); ID `6849` | CDIAL gives Hindi *dhūā̃/dhuwā̃*, Bhojpuri *dhuā̃* and Gujarati *dhūvɔ* from *dhūma*. The weakened m and vowel hiatus are supported; the Bagheli retroflex d variants and some deaspirated forms warrant local phonetic confirmation. |
| 36 | **rākh** ‘ash’ | **rakṣā**, [CDIAL 10552](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=611); ID `10552` | Prakrit *rakkhā*, Hindi *rākh/rākhī* and Gujarati/Marathi *rākh* are listed under the ashes homonym *rakṣā*. The meaning selects this entry, not the separate ‘protection’ homonym. |
| 38 | **dhuḷo, duḷā, dhuro, duḷo, duḷa, dhuḷa, dhuḷ** ‘dust’ | **\*dʰūḍi**, [CDIAL 6835](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=391); ID `6835` | The article groups *dhūli* with *dhūḍi* and explicitly cites Hindi *dhūl/dhūr*, Gujarati *dhūḷ/dhūṛ* and Marathi *dhūḷ*. These substantiate the lateral/rhotic variants; the Bagheli retroflex and deaspirated ḍul remains qualified. |
| 40 | **patta, pattā, patti, patto** ‘leaf’ | **páttra**, [CDIAL 7733](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=437); ID `7733` | Prakrit *patta/pattiā* and Hindi *pattā/pattī* ‘leaf’ are explicit under *pattra*. These dental-stop forms match; Malvi pān and the nasalised or retroflex-looking Nimadi forms require separate analysis. |
| 41 | **phul, ɸul** ‘flower’ | **pʰulla**, [CDIAL 9092](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=511); ID `9092` | CDIAL lists Prakrit *phulla* ‘flower’, Hindi *phūl*, Old Marwari *phūla* and Gujarati/Marathi *phūl*. The survey’s ph/ɸ variants fit this family, distinct from the fruit word *phala*. |
| 42 | **ɸal, phaḷ, phal** ‘fruit’ | **pʰála**, [CDIAL 9051](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=508); ID `9051` | CDIAL gives Hindi *phal*, Gujarati/Marathi *phaḷ*, and eastern *phar*. The ph/ɸ and l/ḷ variants are direct comparanda; Bagheli’s retained l and -ua extension leave Hindi contact or local derivation open. |
| 43 | **keḷa, keḷā, keḷo, kerā, kero, kele, kelā, kela** ‘banana’ | **kadala**, [CDIAL 2712.1](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=136); ID `2712` | The first branch of *kadala/kadalī* has Prakrit *kēla*, Hindi *kelā*, Bhojpuri *kerā* and Gujarati/Marathi *keḷ-* forms. Contraction and lateral/rhotic variation support this branch; the unrelated retroflex-d form *kaḍalī* is not selected. |
| 44 | **kaṭ̃a, kānṭa, kāṭə, kāṭā, kā̃ṭo, kaṭ̃ā, kā̃ṭā, kānḍo, kaṇṭo** ‘thorn’ | **káṇṭaka**, [CDIAL 2668.2](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=133); ID `2668-2` | CDIAL’s *kaṇṭaka* subsection gives Hindi/Marathi *kā̃ṭā*, Marathi *kāṭā*, Gujarati *kā̃ṭɔ* and Sindhi *kaṇḍo*. These substantiate the full-vowel thorn nouns, rather than automatically using the shorter *kaṇṭa* branch; merged ‘bite’ records are excluded. |
| 45 | **vadəḷā, badaḷa, bādaḷā, badəḷā, badəḷ, vadəḷo, vādərā, vadəro, bādəla, baddal, vādəḷa, baddaḷ, bādəl** ‘cloud’ | **vārdala**, [CDIAL 11567](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=674); ID `11567` | CDIAL has Prakrit *vaddala* ‘cloud’, Hindi *bādal/bādar*, Bihari *bādar* and Gujarati/Marathi *vādaḷ*. Initial v/b and lateral/rhotic variants are directly supported; Bagheli’s retroflex d notation remains a phonetic qualification. |

| # | Malvi | Proposed etymology | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 37 | **rakhoḍi, rākhoḍi, rakhoḍo, rakhoḍa** ‘ash’ | **\*rakṣāpuṭaka**, [CDIAL 10555](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=611); ID `10555` | CDIAL reconstructs *rakṣāpuṭaka* specifically for Gujarati *rākhɔṛɔ/rākhɔṛī* ‘layers of ashes, ashes’. This proposes the shared entry family only, retaining both ancestral alternatives. |

# Malvi — batch 004

**13 proposals, approved and saved to the accepted overlay.**

## Straightforward

| # | Malvi | Proposed etymology | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 46 | **gãv, gaũ, ghaũ, gavũ** ‘wheat’ | **gōdʰū́ma**, [CDIAL 4287](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=230); ID `4287` | CDIAL gives Hindi *gehũ/gahũ/gohũ*, Gujarati *gahũ/ghaũ*, Marathi *gahũ* and Konkani *gaṃv* under *godhūma*. The h-retaining and more contracted western forms thus have explicit comparanda; the unrelated village homonym is excluded. |
| 49 | **kando, kāndā, kandā, kanda, kāndo** ‘onion’ | **kanda**, [CDIAL 2723](https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/soas_query.py?page=136); ID `2723` | CDIAL specifies Hindi *kā̃dā*, Gujarati *kā̃dɔ* and Marathi *kā̃dā* ‘onion’ under *kanda* ‘bulbous root’. The source meaning therefore supports this family directly; Nimadi kānta additionally needs confirmation of the devoiced stop. |

| 286 | **ṭamaṭār, ṭamāṭar, ṭamaṭar, ṭemaṭer, ṭāmeṭar, tamaṭār, ṭamāṭer, ṭameṭar** ‘tomato’ | Borrowed from Hindi-Urdu **ṭamaṭar**, kannauji p. 74; [Primary evidence](<https://hi.wiktionary.org/w/index.php?title=टमाटर&oldid=486401#शब्दसागर>); ID `f_ioja36d77utxa` | Borrowing proposed from attested Hindi ṭamaṭar ‘tomato’ (Kannauji survey p. 74). The full Hindi Śabdasāgara entry, read in its explicitly attributed digital reproduction, identifies टमाटर with English tomato; local vowel and dental/retroflex variants remain qualified, and selecting the Hindi donor does not assert direct borrowing from English or an identified donor village. |
